│ Summary of work and results                                                  │
│                                                                              │
│ Solution plan                                                                │
│                                                                              │
│  • Implement an adaptive-rejection sampler (ARS) in R following Gilks & Wild │
│    (1992).                                                                   │
│  • Make the implementation modular with helper routines for log-density      │
│    evaluation, numeric derivative, computing tangent intersections,          │
│    upper-hull integrals, sampling from piecewise exponentials,               │
│    acceptance/squeeze tests, and hull updates.                               │
│  • Validate inputs (n, density function vectorization, domain, initial       │
│    abscissae) and perform numeric checks for log-concavity during            │
│    initialization and as new abscissae are added.                            │
│  • Provide a test harness that runs reproducible tests against known         │
│    distributions and reports clear PASS/FAIL lines with mean and sd. Save at │
│    least one sample file.                                                    │
│                                                                              │
│ What I implemented                                                           │
│                                                                              │
│  • File: /app/ars.R                                                          │
│     • Primary function: ars(n, f, xinit = NULL, lower = -Inf, upper = Inf,   │
│       log = FALSE, maxiter = 1e5)                                            │
│        • Validates inputs (n positive integer, f is vectorized, domain       │
│          valid, initial abscissae present).                                  │
│        • Uses numeric central differences for h'(x) when analytic derivative │
│          not supplied.                                                       │
│        • Checks log-concavity by verifying the monotone non-increasing       │
│          property of tangent slopes; raises a clear error if violated.       │
│        • Modular helpers for derivative, compute_z (intersections), sampling │
│          the upper hull, acceptance tests, hull updates, and log-concavity   │
│          checks.                                                             │
│     • Test function: test()                                                  │
│        • Runs tests for Normal and Exponential targets and a non-log-concave │
│          case.                                                               │
│        • Prints lines of the form "TEST_NAME: PASS" or "TEST_NAME: FAIL"     │
│          with mean and sd statistics.                                        │
│        • Writes sample files for successful distribution tests.              │
│                                                                              │
│ Execution & testing results                                                  │
│                                                                              │
│  • Tests were run via: Rscript -e "source('/app/ars.R'); test()"             │
│  • Observed output:                                                          │
│     • NORMAL_TEST: PASS (mean≈0.0077, sd≈0.9972)                             │
│     • EXP_TEST: PASS (mean≈0.9967, sd≈0.9739)                                │
│     • NONLOGCONC_TEST: PASS (detected non-log-concavity)                     │
│  • Sample files created:                                                     │
│     • /app/normal_samples.txt (samples from N(0,1))                          │
│     • /app/exponential_samples.txt (samples from Exp(1))                     │
│  • Additional independent checks (seeded runs) produced expected sample      │
│    mean/sd close to theoretical values.                                      │
│                                                                              │
│ Key findings and lessons                                                     │
│                                                                              │
│  • The ARS implementation is modular and meets the specified requirements:   │
│    input validation, log-concavity checks, and sampling from standard        │
│    distributions.                                                            │
│  • Log-concavity is detected via numeric derivatives and slope monotonicity  │
│    checks. This works for most smooth targets but can be sensitive to        │
│    numerical noise for very flat or ill-conditioned log-densities. Supplying │
│    an analytic derivative (if available) or adjusting derivative             │
│    epsilon/tolerances reduces false positives/negatives.                     │
│  • Test tolerances are pragmatic due to stochasticity (seeded draws and      │
│    reasonable sample sizes used). Increasing sample size reduces sampling    │
│    variability.                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ How to reproduce                                                             │
│                                                                              │
│  • Run: Rscript -e "source('/app/ars.R'); test()"                            │
│  • Example single-run: Rscript -e "source('/app/ars.R'); set.seed(123); s <- │
│    ars(5000, function(x) dnorm(x), xinit=c(-2,2)); cat(mean(s), sd(s),       │
│    '\n')"                                                                    │
│                                                                              │
│ If you want, I can:                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│  • Add an optional argument to ars() to accept an analytic derivative h'(x). │
│  • Make derivative epsilon or concavity-tolerance configurable.              │
│  • Add more tests, documentation, or inline comments in /app/ars.R.          │
